can straighten out the doctors, the family, and the office, and I start believing my system is better than anyone else’s judgment.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need Like, I used to go on photography walks, help mentor the kids, keep my calendar really organized… and lately all of that just feels flat and heavy. I’m not really starting things the way I normally would, and that’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a specific stressful event or major life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it did start around a specific change. About a year and three months ago, a family member’s health and behavior changed, and I ended up taking on a lot more coordination for things. That’s when I started noticing this shift in myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this flat and heavy way on most days for at least two years?\n\nPatient: No, not two years. It’s been more like about a year and three months, starting after my family member’s health and behavior changed and I had to take on more coordination.\n\nDoctor: When your family member’s situation briefly eases up, does your mood also noticeably improve?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t tracked it that closely. I guess if things calm down for a bit, I might feel a little less pressured, but I can’t say my mood noticeably lifts right away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed most days. I’ve felt kind of flat and weighed down at times, but I haven’t really kept track day by day.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms lasted continuously for more than six months since they started?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been more than six months—about a year and three months now, since my family member’s health and behavior changed and I had to start coordinating more.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms still connected to the ongoing stress of your family member’s situation?\n\nPatient: Yes, I’d say they’re still tied to that. It started after my family member’s health and behavior changed, and once I had to take on more of the coordinating. It’s been going on about a year and three months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. The things that usually make me feel like myself—like going on photography walks, mentoring the kids, even just keeping my calendar organized—started feeling kind of flat and heavy. I noticed I just wasn’t starting them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I’d call it sad. Maybe down sometimes, but it’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything. It’s more just… kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Before this started a year and three months ago, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt this same kind of loss of interest or emotional flatness for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, not really like this. What made me come in was that those things that normally make me feel like myself—my photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean—started feeling flat and heavy, and I stopped starting them on my own. I don’t remember another stretch before this where it was the same for a couple weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this change made it hard for you to keep up with your responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I’m still getting my work done overall, but I’ve noticed I miss small calendar details now, and I have to double-check things that used to be automatic for me. Outside of work, I’ve pulled back from the photography outings and mentoring because I just don’t feel as present or as reliable.\n\nDoctor: Have there been at least two weeks when this loss of interest was present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been there most days for more than two weeks. The photography walks, mentoring, even just keeping my calendar the way I usually do—it all started feeling flat and kind of heavy, and I stopped starting those things on my own.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you felt sad or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had times where I felt flat or worn down, but I don’t know that I’d call it sad or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for two weeks. I haven’t been tracking it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do your reactions to your family member’s situation feel stronger than you would expect for what’s happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, it bothers me, but I don’t know if it’s stronger than what would make sense. I haven’t really compared it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless. I’ve felt kind of flat and tired of things, but hopeless… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has your family member’s situation been ongoing during this whole past year and three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much. It’s been about a year and three months, starting after my family member’s health and behavior changed and I ended up taking on more of the coordination.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this has caused significant distress or seriously interfered with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’d say it’s caused some distress and it’s affected things, but I’m still functioning. Work is mostly intact, but I’ve missed calendar details and I have to double-check things that used to be automatic for me. Outside of work, I’ve pulled back from photography outings and mentoring because I don’t feel as present or as reliable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It started after my family member’s health and behavior changed, and once I had to take on more of the coordinating. It’s been going on about a year and three months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in the things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah. The things that usually make me feel like myself—like going on photography walks, mentoring the kids, even just keeping my calendar organized—started feeling kind of flat and heavy. It’s more just… kind of flat.\n\nDoctor: Before this started a year and three months ago, have you ever had another period in your life when you felt this same kind of loss of interest or emotional flatness for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, not really like this. What made me come in was that those things that normally make me feel like myself—my photography walks, mentoring the kids, even keeping my calendar clean—started feeling flat and heavy, and I stopped starting them on my own. I don’t remember another stretch before this where it was the same for a couple weeks like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this change made it hard for you to keep up with your responsibilities day to day?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I’m still getting my work done overall, but I’ve noticed I miss small calendar details now, and I have to double-check things that used to be automatic for me. The photography walks, mentoring, even just keeping my calendar the way I usually do—it all started feeling flat and kind of heavy, and I stopped starting those things on my own.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you felt sad or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had times where I felt flat or worn down, but I don’t know that I’d call it sad or hopeless most of the day nearly every day for two weeks. I haven’t been tracking it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do your reactions to your family member’s situation feel stronger than you would expect for what’s happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, it bothers me, but I don’t know if it’s stronger than what would make sense. I haven’t really compared it that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it hopeless. I’ve felt kind of flat and tired of things, but hopeless… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has your family member’s situation been ongoing during this whole past year and three months?\n\nPatient: Yes, pretty much. I haven’t been tracking it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do your reactions to your family member’s situation feel stronger than you would expect for what’s happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It’s been about a year and three months, starting after my family member’s health and behavior changed and I ended up taking on more of the coordination.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this has caused significant distress or seriously interfered with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I’d say it’s caused some distress and it’s affected things, but I’m still functioning. Work is mostly intact, but I’ve missed calendar details and I have to double-check things that used to be automatic for me. Outside of work, I’ve pulled back from photography outings and mentoring because I don’t feel as present or as reliable.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period when you felt worthless?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’ve had times where I felt worn down or like I wasn’t doing enough, but I don’t think I’d say I felt worthless.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been at least two weeks when your energy was low nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, there were stretches like that. It was more like things just felt heavy nearly every day. It’s been pretty normal, as far as I can tell."
